title:	[Bug]: MCP OAuth dynamic client registration fails with invalid_redirect_uri — Moltis sends https://localhost instead of http://localhost
state:	OPEN
author:	Maelgk
labels:	bug
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	629
--
### Preflight Checklist

- [x] I have searched [existing issues](https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/issues?q=is%3Aissue+label%3Abug) and this hasn't been reported yet
- [x] I am using the latest version of Moltis
- [ ] If this happened during a chat session, I included as much full session context as possible and redacted secrets

### What happened?

When adding a remote MCP server that requires OAuth 2.1 (e.g. Attio), the dynamic client registration (RFC 7591) fails with invalid_redirect_uri. This affects all remote MCP servers using OAuth, not just Attio.
Root cause: Moltis registers a redirect_uri using https://localhost:<port>/... (its own TLS-enabled address). Many OAuth authorization servers reject https://localhost redirect URIs, as per [RFC 8252 Section 8.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8252#section-8.3) which specifies that native apps should use http://localhost (not https) for loopback redirects.

### Expected behavior

Moltis should use http://localhost:<port>/oauth/callback (not https://) as the redirect URI when performing dynamic client registration for MCP OAuth, consistent with RFC 8252 for native applications.


### Steps to reproduce

Start Moltis (no special config needed)
Go to Settings → MCP Servers → Add Server
Enter https://mcp.attio.com/mcp as the URL
Moltis receives 401, starts OAuth discovery (works fine)
Moltis fetches AS metadata from https://app.attio.com/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server (works fine)
Moltis sends dynamic client registration to https://app.attio.com/oauth/register with an https://localhost redirect URI → 400 Bad Request

### Did this happen during a chat session?

None

### Chat session context (if applicable)

```markdown

```

### Error messages / logs

```shell
INFO  moltis_oauth::discovery: fetched resource metadata resource=https://mcp.attio.com/mcp servers=1
INFO  moltis_oauth::discovery: fetched AS metadata issuer=https://app.attio.com
DEBUG moltis_oauth::registration_store: registration file not found path=...
DEBUG moltis_oauth::discovery: registering dynamic OAuth client endpoint=https://app.attio.com/oauth/register client_name="moltis (attio)"
WARN  moltis_gateway::methods: method error method="mcp.reauth" code=INTERNAL msg=failed to register OAuth client: dynamic client registration returned HTTP 400 Bad Request: {"error":"invalid_redirect_uri"}
```

### Is this a regression?

Not sure

### Last working version

_No response_

### Moltis version

moltis 20260409.01

### Component

Gateway / Web UI

### Install method

Homebrew

### Operating system

macOS (Apple Silicon)

### Additional context

Configuring manual OAuth credentials in moltis.toml does not help — Moltis still performs dynamic client registration instead of using the provided client_id:

[mcp.servers.attio]
url = "https://mcp.attio.com/mcp"
transport = "sse"

[mcp.servers.attio.oauth]
client_id = "manually-registered-client-id"
auth_url = "https://app.attio.com/oauth/authorize"
token_url = "https://app.attio.com/oauth/token"
scopes = ["openid", "offline_access", "mcp"]
```

     1→use std::{collections::HashMap, sync::Arc};
     2→
     3→use {
     4→    axum::{Router, extract::Query, response::Html, routing::get},
     5→    tokio::sync::oneshot,
     6→};
     7→
     8→use crate::{Error, Result};
     9→
    10→/// Starts a local HTTP server to receive the OAuth callback, then shuts down.
    11→pub struct CallbackServer;
    12→
    13→impl CallbackServer {
    14→    /// Listen on `{bind_addr}:{port}` for a GET `/auth/callback` with `code` and `state` params.
    15→    /// Validates state matches `expected_state`, returns the authorization code.
    16→    /// Times out after 60 seconds.
    17→    pub async fn wait_for_code(
    18→        port: u16,
    19→        expected_state: String,
    20→        bind_addr: &str,
    21→    ) -> Result<String> {
    22→        let (tx, rx) = oneshot::channel::<Result<String>>();
    23→        let tx = Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(Some(tx)));
    24→
    25→        let app = Router::new().route(
    26→            "/auth/callback",
    27→            get(move |Query(params): Query<HashMap<String, String>>| {
    28→                let tx = tx.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).take();
    29→                async move {
    30→                    let result = (|| {
    31→                        let state = params.get("state").ok_or("missing state")?;
    32→                        if *state != expected_state {
    33→                            return Err("state mismatch");
    34→                        }
    35→                        let code = params.get("code").ok_or("missing code")?;
    36→                        Ok(code.clone())
    37→                    })();
    38→
    39→                    match result {
    40→                        Ok(code) => {
    41→                            if let Some(tx) = tx {
    42→                                let _ = tx.send(Ok(code));
    43→                            }
    44→                            Html("<h1>Authentication successful!</h1><p>You can close this window.</p>".to_string())
    45→                        }
    46→                        Err(e) => {
    47→                            if let Some(tx) = tx {
    48→                                let _ = tx.send(Err(Error::message(e)));
    49→                            }
    50→                            Html(format!("<h1>Authentication failed</h1><p>{e}</p>"))

     1→//! OAuth 2.1 authentication provider for MCP servers.
     2→//!
     3→//! Implements the MCP Authorization Spec (2025-06-18):
     4→//! - Protected resource metadata discovery (RFC 9728)
     5→//! - Authorization server metadata discovery (RFC 8414)
     6→//! - Dynamic client registration (RFC 7591)
     7→//! - PKCE authorization code flow (web UI callback driven)
     8→
     9→use std::sync::Arc;
    10→
    11→use {
    12→    async_trait::async_trait,
    13→    secrecy::{ExposeSecret, Secret},
    14→    tokio::sync::RwLock,
    15→    tracing::{debug, info, warn},
    16→    url::Url,
    17→};
    18→
    19→use crate::{
    20→    error::{Context, Error, Result},
    21→    remote::sanitize_url_for_display,
    22→};
    23→
    24→use moltis_oauth::{
    25→    OAuthConfig, OAuthFlow, OAuthTokens, RegistrationStore, StoredRegistration, TokenStore,
    26→    fetch_as_metadata, fetch_resource_metadata, parse_www_authenticate, register_client,
    27→};
    28→
    29→// ── Auth state ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    30→
    31→/// Observable state of MCP OAuth authentication.
    32→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
    33→#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
    34→pub enum McpAuthState {
    35→    /// No authentication required or not yet attempted.
    36→    NotRequired,
    37→    /// Browser opened, waiting for user to complete OAuth flow.
    38→    AwaitingBrowser,
    39→    /// Successfully authenticated (have valid tokens).
    40→    Authenticated,
    41→    /// Authentication failed.
    42→    Failed,
    43→}
    44→
    45→// ── Auth provider trait ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    46→
    47→/// Provides OAuth tokens for authenticating MCP HTTP requests.
    48→#[async_trait]
    49→pub trait McpAuthProvider: Send + Sync {
    50→    /// Return a valid access token, refreshing if necessary.
    51→    /// Returns `None` if no token is available and auth hasn't been initiated.
    52→    async fn access_token(&self) -> Result<Option<Secret<String>>>;
    53→
    54→    /// Handle a 401 Unauthorized response by performing the OAuth flow.
    55→    /// Returns `true` if authentication succeeded and the request should be retried.
    56→    async fn handle_unauthorized(&self, www_authenticate: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool>;
    57→
    58→    /// Start an OAuth flow using the provided redirect URI and return an auth URL.
    59→    async fn start_oauth(
    60→        &self,
    61→        redirect_uri: &str,
    62→        www_authenticate: Option<&str>,
    63→    ) -> Result<Option<String>>;
    64→
    65→    /// Complete a pending OAuth flow for this provider.
    66→    ///
    67→    /// Returns `true` when `state` matched and completion was attempted.
    68→    async fn complete_oauth(&self, state: &str, code: &str) -> Result<bool>;
    69→
    70→    /// Pending OAuth authorization URL for this provider, if any.
    71→    fn pending_auth_url(&self) -> Option<String>;
    72→
    73→    /// Current authentication state.
    74→    fn auth_state(&self) -> McpAuthState;
    75→}
    76→
    77→// ── Concrete OAuth provider ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    78→
    79→/// Manual OAuth override configuration (from `moltis.toml`).
    80→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    81→pub struct McpOAuthOverride {
    82→    pub client_id: String,
    83→    pub auth_url: String,
    84→    pub token_url: String,
    85→    pub scopes: Vec<String>,
    86→}
    87→
    88→/// OAuth 2.1 provider for a single MCP server.
    89→pub struct McpOAuthProvider {
    90→    server_name: String,
    91→    server_url: Secret<String>,
    92→    server_url_display: String,
    93→    http_client: reqwest::Client,
    94→    token_store: TokenStore,
    95→    registration_store: RegistrationStore,
    96→    state: RwLock<McpAuthState>,
    97→    cached_token: RwLock<Option<OAuthTokens>>,
    98→    pending_oauth: RwLock<Option<PendingOAuthFlow>>,
    99→    last_www_authenticate: RwLock<Option<String>>,
   100→    /// Optional manual override (skip discovery).

   226→    async fn start_web_oauth_flow(
   227→        &self,
   228→        redirect_uri: &str,
   229→        www_authenticate: Option<&str>,
   230→    ) -> Result<String> {
   231→        let (client_id, auth_url, token_url, scopes, resource) =
   232→            if let Some(ov) = &self.oauth_override {
   233→                // Manual override: skip discovery
   234→                (
   235→                    ov.client_id.clone(),
   236→                    ov.auth_url.clone(),
   237→                    ov.token_url.clone(),
   238→                    ov.scopes.clone(),
   239→                    self.server_url.expose_secret().to_string(),
   240→                )
   241→            } else {
   242→                // Full discovery flow
   243→                // Re-register for each interactive flow so redirect URI always
   244→                // matches the current web origin callback.
   245→                let _ = self
   246→                    .registration_store
   247→                    .delete(self.server_url.expose_secret());
   248→                let header = if let Some(v) = www_authenticate {
   249→                    Some(v.to_string())
   250→                } else {
   251→                    self.last_www_authenticate.read().await.clone()
   252→                };
   253→                self.discover_and_register(header.as_deref(), redirect_uri)
   254→                    .await?
   255→            };
   256→
   257→        let config = OAuthConfig {
   258→            client_id,
   259→            auth_url,
   260→            token_url,
   261→            redirect_uri: redirect_uri.to_string(),
   262→            resource: Some(resource),
   263→            scopes,
   264→            extra_auth_params: Vec::new(),
   265→            device_flow: false,
   266→        };
   267→        let resource_display = config
   268→            .resource
   269→            .as_deref()
   270→            .map(sanitize_url_for_display)
   271→            .unwrap_or_default();
   272→
   273→        info!(
   274→            server = %self.server_name,
   275→            resource = %resource_display,
   276→            "starting MCP OAuth authorization flow"
   277→        );
   278→
   279→        let flow = OAuthFlow::new(config.clone());
   280→        let auth_req = flow.start().context("failed to start OAuth flow")?;
   281→
   282→        *self.pending_oauth.write().await = Some(PendingOAuthFlow {
   283→            state: auth_req.state.clone(),
   284→            verifier: auth_req.pkce.verifier,
   285→            config,
   286→            auth_url: auth_req.url.clone(),
   287→        });
   288→        *self.state.write().await = McpAuthState::AwaitingBrowser;
   289→
   290→        info!(server = %self.server_name, "MCP OAuth authorization URL prepared");
   291→
   292→        Ok(auth_req.url)
   293→    }
   294→
   295→    async fn complete_web_oauth_flow(&self, state: &str, code: &str) -> Result<bool> {
   296→        let pending = {
   297→            let mut slot = self.pending_oauth.write().await;
   298→            let Some(current) = slot.as_ref() else {
   299→                return Ok(false);
   300→            };
   301→            if current.state != state {
   302→                return Ok(false);
   303→            }
   304→            slot.take()
   305→        };
   306→
   307→        let Some(pending) = pending else {
   308→            return Ok(false);
   309→        };
   310→
   311→        let flow = OAuthFlow::new(pending.config);
   312→        let tokens = flow
   313→            .exchange(code, &pending.verifier)
   314→            .await
   315→            .context("OAuth token exchange failed")?;
   316→
   317→        self.token_store
   318→            .save(&self.store_key(), &tokens)
   319→            .context("failed to persist exchanged OAuth tokens")?;
   320→        *self.cached_token.write().await = Some(tokens);
   321→        *self.state.write().await = McpAuthState::Authenticated;
   322→
   323→        info!(server = %self.server_name, "MCP OAuth authentication complete");
   324→
   325→        Ok(true)
   326→    }
   327→
   328→    /// Extract the origin (scheme + host + port) from a URL, stripping the path.
   329→    fn origin_url(url: &Url) -> Url {
   330→        let mut origin = url.clone();
   331→        origin.set_path("/");
   332→        origin.set_query(None);
   333→        origin.set_fragment(None);
   334→        origin
   335→    }
   336→
   337→    /// Build an RFC 8707 resource indicator from a server URL origin.
   338→    ///
   339→    /// Uses scheme + host (+ explicit port) without path/query/fragment.
   340→    fn origin_resource(url: &Url) -> String {
   341→        match (url.host_str(), url.port()) {
   342→            (Some(host), Some(port)) => format!("{}://{host}:{port}", url.scheme()),
   343→            (Some(host), None) => format!("{}://{host}", url.scheme()),
   344→            _ => url.to_string(),
   345→        }
   346→    }
   347→
   348→    /// Discover resource + AS metadata and perform dynamic client registration.
   349→    ///
   350→    /// Returns `(client_id, auth_url, token_url, scopes, resource)`.
   351→    ///
   352→    /// Per the MCP Authorization spec, well-known metadata URLs are tried at the
   353→    /// server's full URL first (path-aware), then at the origin (scheme + host)
   354→    /// as a fallback.
   355→    async fn discover_and_register(
   356→        &self,
   357→        www_authenticate: Option<&str>,
   358→        redirect_uri: &str,
   359→    ) -> Result<(String, String, String, Vec<String>, String)> {
   360→        let server_url = Url::parse(self.server_url.expose_secret())
   361→            .with_context(|| format!("invalid MCP server URL: {}", self.server_url_display))?;
   362→        let origin = Self::origin_url(&server_url);
   363→        let has_path = server_url.path() != "/" && !server_url.path().is_empty();
   364→
   365→        debug!(
   366→            server = %self.server_name,
   367→            server_url = %self.server_url_display,
   368→            origin = %origin,
   369→            has_path,
   370→            www_authenticate = ?www_authenticate,
   371→            "starting OAuth discovery"
   372→        );
   373→
   374→        // Step 1: Try to get resource metadata (RFC 9728) from WWW-Authenticate
   375→        // header or well-known endpoint. If the path-aware URL fails and the
   376→        // server URL has a non-trivial path, retry at the origin.
   377→        let resource_meta_result =
   378→            if let Some(meta_url) = www_authenticate.and_then(parse_www_authenticate) {
   379→                debug!(url = %meta_url, "using resource_metadata URL from WWW-Authenticate");
   380→                let meta_url = Url::parse(&meta_url)
   381→                    .context("invalid resource_metadata URL in WWW-Authenticate header")?;
   382→                fetch_resource_metadata(&self.http_client, &meta_url).await
   383→            } else {
   384→                let result = fetch_resource_metadata(&self.http_client, &server_url).await;
   385→                if result.is_err() && has_path {
   386→                    debug!(
   387→                        server = %self.server_name,
   388→                        origin = %origin,
   389→                        "resource metadata unavailable at path-aware URL, trying origin"
   390→                    );
   391→                    // Try origin; if that also fails, keep the original error
   392→                    fetch_resource_metadata(&self.http_client, &origin)
   393→                        .await
   394→                        .or(result)
   395→                } else {
   396→                    result
   397→                }
   398→            };
   399→
   400→        // Step 2: Get AS metadata — either from resource metadata's
   401→        // authorization_servers list, or directly from the server's origin.
   402→        let (as_meta, resource) = match resource_meta_result {
   403→            Ok(resource_meta) => {
   404→                let resource = resource_meta.resource.clone();
   405→                let as_url_str = resource_meta
   406→                    .authorization_servers
   407→                    .first()
   408→                    .context("no authorization_servers in protected resource metadata")?;
   409→                let as_url = Url::parse(as_url_str)
   410→                    .with_context(|| format!("invalid authorization server URL: {as_url_str}"))?;
   411→                let as_meta = fetch_as_metadata(&self.http_client, &as_url)
   412→                    .await
   413→                    .context("failed to fetch authorization server metadata")?;
   414→                (as_meta, resource)
   415→            },
   416→            Err(e) => {
   417→                debug!(
   418→                    server = %self.server_name,
   419→                    error = %e,
   420→                    "RFC 9728 resource metadata unavailable, trying RFC 8414"
   421→                );
   422→                // Fall back: fetch AS metadata. Try the server URL first, then
   423→                // the origin if the server has a non-trivial path.
   424→                let as_meta =
   425→                    match fetch_as_metadata(&self.http_client, &server_url).await {
   426→                        Ok(meta) => meta,
   427→                        Err(path_err) if has_path => {
   428→                            debug!(
   429→                                server = %self.server_name,
   430→                                origin = %origin,
   431→                                "AS metadata unavailable at path-aware URL, trying origin"
   432→                            );
   433→                            fetch_as_metadata(&self.http_client, &origin).await.with_context(|| {
   434→                            format!(
   435→                                "AS metadata unavailable at both {} and {origin}: {path_err}",
   436→                                self.server_url_display
   437→                            )
   438→                        })?
   439→                        },
   440→                        Err(e) => {
   441→                            return Err(Error::message(format!(
   442→                                "failed to fetch authorization server metadata: {e}"
   443→                            )));
   444→                        },
   445→                    };
   446→                // When resource metadata is unavailable we fall back to origin
   447→                // as the resource indicator to avoid path-scoped audience mismatches.
   448→                let resource = Self::origin_resource(&server_url);
   449→                (as_meta, resource)
   450→            },
   451→        };
   452→
   453→        debug!(
   454→            server = %self.server_name,
   455→            issuer = %as_meta.issuer,
   456→            auth_endpoint = %as_meta.authorization_endpoint,
   457→            token_endpoint = %as_meta.token_endpoint,
   458→            registration = ?as_meta.registration_endpoint,
   459→            resource = %sanitize_url_for_display(&resource),
   460→            "resolved OAuth endpoints"
   461→        );
   462→
   463→        // Step 3: Dynamic client registration (or use cached)
   464→        let client_id = if let Some(cached) = self
   465→            .registration_store
   466→            .load(self.server_url.expose_secret())
   467→        {
   468→            debug!(
   469→                server = %self.server_name,
   470→                client_id = %cached.client_id,
   471→                "reusing cached dynamic registration"
   472→            );
   473→            cached.client_id
   474→        } else if let Some(reg_endpoint) = &as_meta.registration_endpoint {
   475→            // Register the exact callback URI that we'll use for this auth flow.
   476→            // Some providers require an exact redirect URI match and reject
   477→            // port-agnostic loopback registrations.
   478→            let reg = register_client(
   479→                &self.http_client,
   480→                reg_endpoint,
   481→                vec![redirect_uri.to_string()],
   482→                &format!("moltis ({})", self.server_name),
   483→            )
   484→            .await
   485→            .context("failed to register OAuth client")?;
   486→
   487→            // Persist registration
   488→            let stored = StoredRegistration {
   489→                client_id: reg.client_id.clone(),
   490→                client_secret: reg.client_secret.map(Secret::new),
   491→                authorization_endpoint: as_meta.authorization_endpoint.clone(),
   492→                token_endpoint: as_meta.token_endpoint.clone(),
   493→                resource: resource.clone(),
   494→                registered_at: std::time::SystemTime::now()
   495→                    .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
   496→                    .map(|d| d.as_secs())
   497→                    .unwrap_or(0),
   498→            };
   499→            self.registration_store
   500→                .save(self.server_url.expose_secret(), &stored)
   501→                .context("failed to persist OAuth registration")?;
   502→
   503→            reg.client_id
   504→        } else {
   505→            return Err(Error::message(
   506→                "AS does not support dynamic client registration and no client_id configured",
   507→            ));
   508→        };
   509→
   510→        Ok((
   511→            client_id,
   512→            as_meta.authorization_endpoint,
   513→            as_meta.token_endpoint,
   514→            as_meta.scopes_supported,
   515→            resource,
   516→        ))
   517→    }
   518→}
   519→

# Issue #629: Plan for fix

## Root cause

`crates/web/src/assets/js/page-mcp.js:105-107` builds the OAuth redirect URI from `window.location.origin`, and Moltis serves the web UI over HTTPS. That produces `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback`, which is then plumbed through:

1. **UI → gateway** via `mcp.oauth.start` / `mcp.add` / `mcp.enable` (`crates/gateway/src/mcp_service.rs:358-617`)
2. **Gateway → manager** as `redirect_uri` arg to `oauth_start_server` / `reauth_server`
3. **`McpOAuthProvider::start_web_oauth_flow`** (`crates/mcp/src/auth.rs:226-293`) where it's
   - Used as the sole entry in `redirect_uris` for **dynamic client registration** (RFC 7591), line 478-483
   - Used as the `redirect_uri` in the **authorization request**

AS's that follow RFC 8252 §8.3 strictly (like Attio's) reject the `https://` scheme for loopback redirects with `invalid_redirect_uri`, causing the registration to fail with HTTP 400.

A secondary complaint in the report — that manually specifying `[mcp.servers.attio.oauth] client_id = …` in `moltis.toml` is ignored — *looks* wrong from `manager.rs:128-146` which does plumb the override. Needs verification; may be a separate bug (the user config may be on a section that isn't being read for remote SSE servers).

## Design decision

Two viable fixes:

**A. Loopback HTTP callback server (RFC 8252 native-app pattern)** — spin up an ephemeral HTTP listener on `127.0.0.1:<random-port>` per OAuth flow, using the existing `crates/oauth/src/callback_server.rs::CallbackServer::wait_for_code`. Register/authorize with `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/auth/callback`. When the AS redirects the browser there, the embedded server receives the code, completes the flow in-process, and shuts down.

**B. Rewrite the web origin to `http://` and run a tiny plain-HTTP listener on the gateway just for `/auth/callback`.** More invasive — the gateway's listener is TLS-only today.

I recommend **A**. Pros:
- Strictly RFC 8252 / RFC 8252 §8.3 compliant
- Uses infra that already exists (`CallbackServer`)
- Works regardless of web UI scheme/port
- Decouples MCP OAuth completion from the web UI being open (currently the `window.close()` trick requires the same browser)
- No change to the gateway's TLS listener

Con: the browser must be able to reach `127.0.0.1` on the Moltis host. Already true for every current deployment (web UI itself is loopback-bound), so no regression.

## Implementation plan

### 1. `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`
- Add a new code path `start_loopback_oauth_flow(www_authenticate)` that:
  1. Binds a `TcpListener` on `127.0.0.1:0` to pick a free port, keeps the port, drops the listener (pass the port to `CallbackServer`).
     - Or add a `CallbackServer::bind_random` variant that returns `(port, Future)` to avoid the race.
  2. Builds `redirect_uri = format!("http://127.0.0.1:{port}/auth/callback")`.
  3. Calls `discover_and_register(header, &redirect_uri)` (unchanged).
  4. Builds the `OAuthConfig` + `OAuthFlow`, obtains `auth_req`.
  5. Stores `PendingOAuthFlow`, sets state `AwaitingBrowser`.
  6. `tokio::spawn`s a task: `CallbackServer::wait_for_code(port, state, "127.0.0.1")`, then on success invokes `self.complete_web_oauth_flow(state, code)` via an `Arc<Self>` (the provider is already `SharedAuthProvider = Arc<dyn McpAuthProvider>`; we need a concrete handle — simplest is to refactor `McpOAuthProvider` methods to be callable on `Arc<Self>`, or pass in a completion closure from `McpManager::oauth_start_server`).
  7. Returns `auth_req.url`.
- Keep `start_web_oauth_flow` around as a fallback (or delete it and always use loopback — see Open Question 1).
- Update the `start_oauth` trait impl (line 574-583) to route to the loopback variant and ignore the incoming `redirect_uri` for MCP (it's now chosen server-side).

### 2. `crates/oauth/src/callback_server.rs`
- Add `CallbackServer::bind_random(expected_state)` → `(port, impl Future<Output=Result<String>>)` so we atomically reserve the port before returning it to the AS. Current `wait_for_code(port, …)` has a TOCTOU gap where the port could be taken between check and bind.
- Ensure the returned HTML and CSP are suitable (already are).

### 3. `crates/mcp/src/manager.rs`
- `oauth_start_server(name, _redirect_uri)` — the `redirect_uri` argument becomes unused for the MCP path (or we keep it and let the provider ignore it when using loopback). Add a deprecation note; preserve the signature for compatibility with callers.
- `oauth_complete_callback` still works as a fallback path; no change required.

### 4. `crates/gateway/src/mcp_service.rs`
- `add`, `enable`, `oauth_start`, `reauth` — no behavior change required (they still pass `redirectUri` through), but the UI can stop sending it (see step 6). Keep the parameter optional for backward compat.

### 5. `crates/web/src/oauth.rs`
- `oauth_callback_handler` — still needed for provider OAuth (`provider_setup.oauth_complete`). Leave as-is. Its MCP branch becomes dead code once the loopback flow lands but can be kept for fallback until removed in a follow-up.

### 6. `crates/web/src/assets/js/page-mcp.js`
- Stop sending `redirectUri` on `mcp.add` / `mcp.enable` / `mcp.oauth.start` / `mcp.reauth`. Gateway picks it.
- `oauthCallbackUrl()` helper can be deleted.
- Run `biome check --write` after the change.

### 7. Tests
- **`crates/mcp/src/auth.rs` unit tests**: existing `dynamic_registration_uses_exact_redirect_uri` already asserts the registration uses `http://127.0.0.1:...`. Add a new test that exercises the full loopback flow using a mock AS + `CallbackServer`, asserting:
  - The registration body contains a `redirect_uri` with scheme `http` and host `127.0.0.1`.
  - `start_oauth` returns a URL whose `redirect_uri` query param also matches.
  - Simulating the AS redirecting to the loopback URL completes the flow and transitions to `Authenticated`.
- **`crates/oauth` unit tests**: test `CallbackServer::bind_random` returns a listening port and completes on a valid `code`/`state` GET.
- **E2E**: `crates/web/ui/e2e/start-gateway-oauth.sh` and `mock-oauth-server.js` already exist. Extend an existing MCP OAuth e2e spec (or add one) that points Moltis at the mock AS and asserts `mcp.oauth.start` produces an auth URL whose `redirect_uri` is `http://127.0.0.1:…`.

### 8. Docs
- `docs/src/...` — brief note under MCP configuration that remote MCP OAuth uses an ephemeral loopback HTTP port for the redirect URI (not the gateway HTTPS port). Mention that the host firewall must allow inbound loopback connections on ephemeral ports.

### 9. Secondary bug (manual OAuth override ignored)
- File a separate `bd create` issue to investigate. Reproduce with the exact TOML from the report; trace `build_auth_provider` call path for remote SSE servers and verify `McpServerConfig::oauth` is actually populated from `[mcp.servers.*.oauth]`. This is out of scope for the #629 fix but should be tracked.

## Open questions

1. **Keep the web-origin callback path for MCP at all?** Dropping it simplifies the code but prevents a user from pasting the callback URL manually (onboarding views currently show a manual paste field). I lean toward keeping the handler (for provider OAuth it's still used) but retiring the MCP code path in a follow-up.
2. **Port range / permissions.** Should we bind strictly to `127.0.0.1` or allow `::1` fallback for IPv6-only hosts? Recommend `127.0.0.1` only (matches what most AS's whitelist).
3. **Timeout.** `CallbackServer::wait_for_code` times out after 60s — probably OK. Expose as a const or config?

## Validation commands

- `just format-check`
- `just lint`
- `just test` (or `cargo nextest run -p moltis-mcp -p moltis-oauth -p moltis-gateway`)
- `cd crates/web/ui && npx biome check --write` (after JS edits)
- `cd crates/web/ui && npx playwright test e2e/specs/mcp-oauth.spec.js` (new/updated spec)
- `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR>` before shipping

> DEVELOPER

The issue I see is, what about people running moltis on a specific IP or host? Like I run mine on https://moltis.lan

> AGENT

Good catch — that's a real hole in plan A. Let me revise.

## Key distinction

RFC 8252 §8.3 only applies to **loopback redirects**: `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or `http://[::1]:<port>`. It's what public/native clients use to avoid needing a fixed redirect.

For a confidential web client on a real hostname — `https://moltis.lan/auth/callback`, `https://moltis.example.com/auth/callback` — the §8.3 loopback rules **don't apply**. Those are just normal web app redirects. Attio and other strict AS's will accept them because the rejection rule is specifically "no `https://localhost`" (and sometimes "no `https://127.0.0.1`"). A real TLS hostname is fine.

So the bug is only real when Moltis is running on a loopback origin. Your `https://moltis.lan` setup works today (or *should*) — the Attio error only fires because `window.location.origin` resolves to `https://localhost:PORT` on the reporter's single-host setup.

## Revised fix: branch on origin

In `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`'s web OAuth entry point, classify the incoming `redirect_uri`:

```rust
enum RedirectStrategy {
    // Host is 127.0.0.1 / ::1 / localhost → spin up ephemeral HTTP loopback callback.
    Loopback,
    // Host is a real name or non-loopback IP → reuse the URI as-is.
    WebOrigin(String),
}
```

Logic:

```rust
let parsed = Url::parse(redirect_uri)?;
let host = parsed.host_str().unwrap_or("");
let is_loopback = match host {
    "localhost" => true,
    h => h.parse::<IpAddr>().map(|ip| ip.is_loopback()).unwrap_or(false),
};
```

- **Loopback branch** (fixes #629): ignore the inbound `redirect_uri`, bind `127.0.0.1:0`, construct `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/auth/callback`, run `CallbackServer::wait_for_code` on a spawned task, complete the flow in-process (as in the original plan).
- **WebOrigin branch** (your `moltis.lan` setup): keep today's behavior unchanged — register and authorize with `https://moltis.lan/auth/callback`, rely on `crates/web/src/oauth.rs::oauth_callback_handler` to receive the redirect and drive completion.

This is backward compatible. Hostname users see zero change; loopback users get the RFC 8252 fix.

## What changes vs. the prior plan

- `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`:
  - Keep `start_web_oauth_flow` as the WebOrigin path (unchanged).
  - Add `start_loopback_oauth_flow` for the loopback path (new).
  - Add a small `redirect_strategy(uri: &str)` helper that selects between them.
  - `McpAuthProvider::start_oauth` picks the strategy based on the URL the UI sent.
- `crates/oauth/src/callback_server.rs`: add `bind_random` (atomic port reservation), as before.
- `crates/gateway/src/mcp_service.rs`: unchanged — keep receiving `redirectUri`.
- `crates/web/src/assets/js/page-mcp.js`: **keep sending `${window.location.origin}/auth/callback`**. Do NOT drop it — the gateway needs it to know the web origin for the WebOrigin branch.
- `crates/web/src/oauth.rs`: unchanged. Its MCP branch stays live for hostname deployments.

## Tests

- Existing `dynamic_registration_uses_exact_redirect_uri` test still passes (covers loopback path).
- New unit test: passing `https://moltis.lan/auth/callback` ends up in `start_web_oauth_flow` and registers *that* URL verbatim.
- New unit test: passing `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback` causes loopback fallback → registration body contains `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.
- New unit test: `https://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback` and `https://[::1]:1455/auth/callback` also hit loopback fallback.
- E2E: extend existing MCP OAuth spec with both a loopback scenario and a `127.0.0.1` hostname rewrite.

## Edge cases to watch

1. **User on `https://localhost` but accessing via port-forward from another host** — rare, but the browser would receive the redirect to `127.0.0.1:<ephemeral>` and try to connect on its own machine. The port forward won't cover the ephemeral port. Acceptable: document that MCP OAuth requires either a real TLS hostname or single-host loopback.
2. **IPv6-only hosts** — `127.0.0.1` won't bind. Should we try `::1` as a fallback? Recommend: try v4 first, fall back to v6 on bind failure.
3. **`0.0.0.0` bound but browsed from LAN** — if the user serves on `https://0.0.0.0` and browses from elsewhere, `window.location.origin` will be the LAN URL, which hits the WebOrigin branch. Works.
4. **Web UI over HTTP (`--no-tls`)** — origin becomes `http://localhost:…`. Still classified loopback → loopback path. Still fine.

Does this approach look right? If so I'll file a `bd` issue and start implementation.

   555→async fn send_http_redirect(
   556→    mut stream: TcpStream,
   557→    https_port: u16,
   558→    bind_host: &str,
   559→    localhost_mode: bool,
   560→) -> Result<()> {
   561→    // Read enough to parse the request line and Host header.
   562→    let mut buf = vec![0u8; 4096];
   563→    let n = tokio::time::timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), stream.peek(&mut buf))
   564→        .await
   565→        .context("timeout reading HTTP request")?
   566→        .context("reading HTTP request")?;
   567→
   568→    let raw = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..n]);
   569→
   570→    // Parse request path from first line: "GET /path HTTP/1.x"
   571→    let path = raw
   572→        .lines()
   573→        .next()
   574→        .and_then(|line| line.split_whitespace().nth(1))
   575→        .unwrap_or("/");
   576→
   577→    // Parse Host header, stripping port if present.
   578→    let host = if localhost_mode {
   579→        "localhost".to_string()
   580→    } else {
   581→        raw.lines()
   582→            .find_map(|line| {
   583→                if line.get(..5)?.eq_ignore_ascii_case("host:") {
   584→                    let value = line[5..].trim();
   585→                    // Use Authority parser to correctly handle IPv6, ports, etc.
   586→                    if let Ok(authority) = value.parse::<axum::http::uri::Authority>() {
   587→                        Some(authority.host().to_string())
   588→                    } else {
   589→                        Some(value.to_string())
   590→                    }
   591→                } else {
   592→                    None
   593→                }
   594→            })
   595→            .unwrap_or_else(|| bind_host.to_string())
   596→    };
   597→
   598→    let location = format!("https://{}:{}{}", format_url_host(&host), https_port, path);
   599→    let body =
   600→        format!("<html><body>Redirecting to <a href=\"{location}\">{location}</a></body></html>");
   601→    let response = format!(
   602→        "HTTP/1.1 301 Moved Permanently\r\n\
   603→         Location: {location}\r\n\
   604→         Content-Type: text/html\r\n\
   605→         Content-Length: {}\r\n\
   606→         Connection: close\r\n\
   607→         \r\n\
   608→         {body}",
   609→        body.len()
   610→    );
   611→
   612→    stream.write_all(response.as_bytes()).await?;
   613→    stream.shutdown().await?;
   614→    Ok(())
   615→}

> AGENT

Yes — confirmed. Moltis's main TLS port has a **peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect** (`crates/tls/src/lib.rs:472-615`): if the first byte isn't a TLS handshake (0x16), it parses the request line and Host header, sends a 301 to `https://<host>:<port>/<path+query>`, and shuts down. Path **and** query string are preserved. `/auth/callback` is unauthenticated (`auth_middleware.rs:222`), so no cookie/session issue.

This makes the fix trivial. We don't need loopback servers, extra ports, or CallbackServer changes.

## Revised plan (simpler)

**Flow**
1. Register `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback` with the AS (instead of `https://…`). RFC 8252 §7.3 permits `localhost` in loopback redirects.
2. AS redirects the user's browser to `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback?code=…&state=…`.
3. Moltis's TLS port receives plain HTTP, peek code detects it, 301s to `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback?code=…&state=…` (path + query preserved).
4. Browser follows → hits existing `oauth_callback_handler` over TLS → flow completes normally.

No change to the callback handler, gateway service, manager, or CallbackServer. Only one small change: rewrite the redirect URI's **scheme** from `https` → `http` when the host is loopback-ish.

## Code changes

### 1. `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs` — scheme normalizer

Add a small helper and call it at the top of `start_web_oauth_flow`, before discovery/registration and before building `OAuthConfig`:

```rust
fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {
    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else { return uri.to_string() };
    let is_loopback = match parsed.host() {
        Some(url::Host::Domain(h)) => h.eq_ignore_ascii_case("localhost"),
        Some(url::Host::Ipv4(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),
        Some(url::Host::Ipv6(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),
        None => false,
    };
    if is_loopback && parsed.scheme() == "https" {
        let _ = parsed.set_scheme("http");
    }
    parsed.to_string()
}
```

That's the whole fix. Real hostnames (`moltis.lan`, `moltis.example.com`) are untouched and continue to use `https://…/auth/callback` as today (which works for them because they don't hit §8.3). Only loopback deployments get the rewrite.

### 2. Tests in `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`

- **Unit:** `normalize_loopback_redirect` matrix:
  - `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback` → `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback` ✓
  - `https://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback` → `http://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback` ✓
  - `https://[::1]:1455/auth/callback` → `http://[::1]:1455/auth/callback` ✓
  - `https://moltis.lan/auth/callback` → unchanged ✓
  - `http://localhost/auth/callback` → unchanged ✓
  - garbage / unparseable → unchanged ✓
- **Integration:** extend `dynamic_registration_uses_exact_redirect_uri` (line 945) with a second case: pass `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback`, assert the registration body contains `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback` (scheme flipped) and that the `redirect_uri` in the auth URL matches.

### 3. E2E (optional but recommended)

`crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/` — if an MCP-OAuth spec exists, add an assertion that `mcp.oauth.start` returns an auth URL whose `redirect_uri` query param is `http://localhost:...`. Otherwise skip; the unit tests cover the critical invariant.

## Things to verify during implementation

1. **Cert trust:** the HTTP→HTTPS bounce lands on the same origin the user already visited (otherwise they wouldn't be using the web UI), so the self-signed CA is already trusted. No new warning.
2. **Port preservation:** `send_http_redirect` reads the Host header and passes through the TLS port — confirmed, line 598.
3. **Secondary HTTP redirect server on `port+1`:** irrelevant here. The AS redirects to whatever `redirect_uri` was registered (the main TLS port), not port+1.
4. **`oauth_callback_handler` path is unauthenticated:** confirmed (`auth_middleware.rs:222`). No cookie needed to complete the flow.
5. **Provider OAuth (`provider-oauth.js`)** has the identical `${window.location.origin}/auth/callback` pattern and the same bug in theory. Out of scope for #629, but I'll file a follow-up `bd` to apply the same normalizer to `provider_setup`'s flow if it performs dynamic registration.

## Out-of-scope follow-ups (separate `bd` issues)

- **"Manual OAuth override ignored"** sub-complaint in #629 — investigate whether `[mcp.servers.<name>.oauth]` actually reaches `build_auth_provider` for SSE remotes.
- Same loopback-scheme normalization for provider OAuth, if/when we find an AS that rejects it there too.

## Validation commands

- `just format-check && just lint`
- `cargo nextest run -p moltis-mcp`
- `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR>`

---

  "old_string": "    async fn start_web_oauth_flow(\n        &self,\n        redirect_uri: &str,\n        www_authenticate: Option<&str>,\n    ) -> Result<String> {\n        let (client_id, auth_url, token_url, scopes, resource) =\n            if let Some(ov) = &self.oauth_override {\n                // Manual override: skip discovery\n                (\n                    ov.client_id.clone(),\n                    ov.auth_url.clone(),\n                    ov.token_url.clone(),\n                    ov.scopes.clone(),\n                    self.server_url.expose_secret().to_string(),\n                )\n            } else {\n                // Full discovery flow\n                // Re-register for each interactive flow so redirect URI always\n                // matches the current web origin callback.\n                let _ = self\n                    .registration_store\n                    .delete(self.server_url.expose_secret());\n                let header = if let Some(v) = www_authenticate {\n                    Some(v.to_string())\n                } else {\n                    self.last_www_authenticate.read().await.clone()\n                };\n                self.discover_and_register(header.as_deref(), redirect_uri)\n                    .await?\n            };\n\n        let config = OAuthConfig {\n            client_id,\n            auth_url,\n            token_url,\n            redirect_uri: redirect_uri.to_string(),\n            resource: Some(resource),\n            scopes,\n            extra_auth_params: Vec::new(),\n            device_flow: false,\n        };",
  "new_string": "    async fn start_web_oauth_flow(\n        &self,\n        redirect_uri: &str,\n        www_authenticate: Option<&str>,\n    ) -> Result<String> {\n        // RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3: loopback redirect URIs must use the `http`\n        // scheme. Many authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n        // `https://localhost` with `invalid_redirect_uri`. Moltis serves the\n        // web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived callback arrives as\n        // `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`. We rewrite the scheme for\n        // loopback hosts; the TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect\n        // (see `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently\n        // bounces the browser back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n        // preserving path and query string.\n        let redirect_uri = normalize_loopback_redirect(redirect_uri);\n        let redirect_uri = redirect_uri.as_str();\n\n        let (client_id, auth_url, token_url, scopes, resource) =\n            if let Some(ov) = &self.oauth_override {\n                // Manual override: skip discovery\n                (\n                    ov.client_id.clone(),\n                    ov.auth_url.clone(),\n                    ov.token_url.clone(),\n                    ov.scopes.clone(),\n                    self.server_url.expose_secret().to_string(),\n                )\n            } else {\n                // Full discovery flow\n                // Re-register for each interactive flow so redirect URI always\n                // matches the current web origin callback.\n                let _ = self\n                    .registration_store\n                    .delete(self.server_url.expose_secret());\n                let header = if let Some(v) = www_authenticate {\n                    Some(v.to_string())\n                } else {\n                    self.last_www_authenticate.read().await.clone()\n                };\n                self.discover_and_register(header.as_deref(), redirect_uri)\n                    .await?\n            };\n\n        let config = OAuthConfig {\n            client_id,\n            auth_url,\n            token_url,\n            redirect_uri: redirect_uri.to_string(),\n            resource: Some(resource),\n            scopes,\n            extra_auth_params: Vec::new(),\n            device_flow: false,\n        };"
}

  "new_string": "        Ok((\n            client_id,\n            as_meta.authorization_endpoint,\n            as_meta.token_endpoint,\n            as_meta.scopes_supported,\n            resource,\n        ))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs so dynamic\n/// client registration (RFC 7591) is accepted by authorization servers that\n/// enforce RFC 8252 §8.3. Non-loopback hosts and non-`https` schemes are\n/// returned unchanged.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's HTTP peek/redirect bounces the plain-HTTP OAuth\n/// callback back onto the real HTTPS handler transparently, so this rewrite\n/// is safe for any deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback\n/// host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\nfn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    let is_loopback = match parsed.host() {\n        Some(url::Host::Domain(host)) => host.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\"),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv4(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv6(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        None => false,\n    };\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    if parsed.set_scheme(\"http\").is_err() {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    parsed.to_string()\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl McpAuthProvider for McpOAuthProvider {"
}

{
  "command": "git add crates/mcp/src/auth.rs && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(mcp): normalize loopback redirect URIs to http for OAuth registration\n\nRFC 8252 §8.3 mandates the `http` scheme for loopback redirect URIs.\nStrict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject `https://localhost`\nduring dynamic client registration (RFC 7591) with `invalid_redirect_uri`,\nbreaking MCP OAuth on single-host deployments where the web UI is served\nover TLS on `https://localhost:<port>`.\n\nRewrite the scheme from `https` to `http` when the redirect URI host is\na loopback identifier (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`). The main TLS\nlistener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect in `moltis_tls` transparently\nbounces the plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback\nhandler, preserving path and query string, so the flow completes without\nany additional listener, gateway, or UI changes.\n\nNon-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) are untouched and\ncontinue to use the web-origin HTTPS callback, which is a legitimate\nconfidential-client redirect URI unaffected by §8.3.\n\nTests: 10 unit tests covering the normalizer matrix (localhost, IPv4/IPv6\nloopback, case-insensitivity, real hostnames, non-loopback IPv4, http\nscheme passthrough, path/query preservation, unparseable input) plus an\nintegration test that drives `start_web_oauth_flow` with a mockito AS and\nasserts the registration body and authorization URL use the rewritten\nHTTP form.\n\nFixes #629\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -15",
  "description": "Commit the fix"
}

> AGENT

Done. Committed as `033bb5fc`.

## Summary

**Fix for #629** — single-file change to `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`:

- Added `normalize_loopback_redirect()` helper that rewrites `https://` → `http://` when the redirect URI host is `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or `::1`. Everything else is returned unchanged.
- Called at the top of `McpOAuthProvider::start_web_oauth_flow`, so both dynamic client registration and the authorization request use the RFC 8252-compliant form.
- No changes to gateway, manager, web UI, or callback handler — the existing TLS peek/HTTP-redirect in `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect` transparently bounces the plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS handler (path + query preserved).
- Your `https://moltis.lan` setup is untouched: non-loopback hosts keep the existing HTTPS redirect URI.

**Tests:** 11 new (10 normalizer units + 1 `start_web_oauth_flow` integration test via mockito). All 87 `moltis-mcp` tests pass. `cargo fmt`, clippy `-D warnings`, and `cargo test` all clean.

**Out of scope (tracked separately if needed):**
- The sub-complaint that `[mcp.servers.*.oauth]` manual override is "ignored" — `build_auth_provider` looks correct at first glance; needs a dedicated repro.
- Same potential issue in `provider-oauth.js` for provider (non-MCP) OAuth.

Not pushing — branch `boulder-penalty` has no upstream (ephemeral per your setup).

   182→    pub async fn start_server(&self, name: &str, config: &McpServerConfig) -> Result<()> {
   183→        // Shut down existing connection if any.
   184→        self.stop_server(name).await;
   185→
   186→        // Network work happens outside the lock.
   187→        let (client, auth_provider) = match config.transport {

   200→                };
   201→
   202→                let has_existing_auth_provider = existing_auth.is_some();

   204→                    Self::build_auth_provider(name, &remote, config.oauth.as_ref())
   205→                });
   206→
   207→                // If we have a stored token, prefer auth transport immediately.
   208→                // This avoids forced re-auth at process start for OAuth-backed servers.
   209→                let has_stored_token = if has_existing_auth_provider {
   210→                    false
   211→                } else {
   212→                    auth_provider.access_token().await?.is_some()
   213→                };
   214→
   215→                if Self::should_attempt_auth_connection(
   216→                    has_existing_auth_provider,
   217→                    config.oauth.is_some(),
   218→                    has_stored_token,
   219→                ) {
   220→                    let client = McpClient::connect_sse_with_auth(

   225→                    )
   226→                    .await?;
   227→                    (client, Some(auth_provider))
   228→                } else {
   229→                    // No hint that auth is needed yet, probe unauthenticated first.
   230→                    match McpClient::connect_sse(name, &remote, self.effective_timeout_for(config))

   255→                                    }
   256→                                    .into());
   257→                                }
   258→
   259→                                // Retry with auth.
   260→                                let client = McpClient::connect_sse_with_auth(
   261→                                    name,

   263→                                    auth_provider.clone(),

   310→    /// Stop a server connection.
   311→    pub async fn stop_server(&self, name: &str) {
   312→        // Atomically remove client and tools, then drop the lock before async shutdown.
   313→        // Keep auth_providers for potential reconnection.
   314→        let client = {
   315→            let mut inner = self.inner.write().await;
   316→            inner.tools.remove(name);
   317→            inner.clients.remove(name)
   318→        };
   319→        if let Some(client) = client {
   320→            let mut c = client.write().await;
   321→            c.shutdown().await;
   322→        }
   323→    }
   324→
   325→    /// Restart a server.
   326→    pub async fn restart_server(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   327→        let config = {
   328→            let inner = self.inner.read().await;
   329→            inner
   330→                .registry
   331→                .get(name)
   332→                .cloned()
   333→                .with_context(|| format!("MCP server '{name}' not found in registry"))?
   334→        };
   335→        self.start_server(name, &config).await
   336→    }

   341→            {
   342→                let inner = self.inner.read().await;

   344→                    McpManagerError::ServerNotFound {

   346→                    }

   348→            };

   352→            TransportType::Sse | TransportType::StreamableHttp
   353→        ) {
   354→            return Err(McpManagerError::NotRemoteTransport {
   355→                server: name.to_string(),
   356→            }

```

> AGENT

Two potential follow-ups I identified:

1. **`provider-oauth.js`** uses the same `${window.location.origin}/auth/callback` pattern. Some providers without a pre-registered redirect URI pass it through `oauth_start`'s `use_server_callback` branch. Applying the same normalization is cheap and consistent — worth fixing.
2. **Manual `[mcp.servers.*.oauth]` override** — the code path in `manager.rs::build_auth_provider` and `auth.rs::start_web_oauth_flow` looks correct (the override branch at line ~242 skips discovery entirely). I can't reproduce the user's claim without running Moltis against Attio. Better to file a tracking issue than attempt a blind fix.

I'll share the `normalize_loopback_redirect` helper by promoting it to `moltis_oauth`, apply it in provider-setup, and file a tracking bd for the override investigation.

  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/boulder-penalty/crates/oauth/src/redirect.rs",
  "content": "//! Redirect URI normalization helpers.\n//!\n//! Centralizes the RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 loopback rewrite so both MCP OAuth\n//! (RFC 7591 dynamic client registration) and provider OAuth produce\n//! authorization-server-compatible URIs regardless of the scheme the\n//! browser started on.\n\nuse url::Url;\n\n/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs.\n///\n/// RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandate that loopback redirect URIs use the `http`\n/// scheme. Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with\n/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization\n/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived\n/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see\n/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the\n/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any\n/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host\n/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\n///\n/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`\n/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect\n/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is\n/// unaffected by §8.3.\n#[must_use]\npub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    let is_loopback = match parsed.host() {\n        Some(url::Host::Domain(host)) => host.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\"),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv4(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv6(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        None => false,\n    };\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    if parsed.set_scheme(\"http\").is_err() {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    parsed.to_string()\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rewrites_https_localhost() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://localhost:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rewrites_https_localhost_default_port() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://localhost/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://localhost/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rewrites_https_ipv4_loopback() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rewrites_https_ipv6_loopback() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://[::1]:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://[::1]:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rewrites_https_localhost_case_insensitive() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://LocalHost:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn preserves_real_hostname() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://moltis.lan/auth/callback\"),\n            \"https://moltis.lan/auth/callback\",\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://moltis.example.com:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"https://moltis.example.com:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn preserves_non_loopback_ipv4() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://192.168.1.10:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"https://192.168.1.10:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn preserves_http_scheme() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback\"),\n            \"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn preserves_query_and_path() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            normalize_loopback_redirect(\"https://localhost:1455/auth/callback?foo=bar\"),\n            \"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback?foo=bar\",\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn returns_unparseable_input_unchanged() {\n        assert_eq!(normalize_loopback_redirect(\"not a url\"), \"not a url\");\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "        // RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3: loopback redirect URIs must use `http`.\n        // The UI sends `${window.location.origin}/auth/callback`, which is\n        // `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback` on TLS-enabled\n        // deployments. Rewrite loopback origins to `http` so strict\n        // authorization servers accept them; the TLS listener's HTTP peek\n        // redirect bounces the callback back onto the HTTPS handler.\n        let redirect_uri = params\n            .get(\"redirectUri\")\n            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())\n            .map(str::trim)\n            .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())\n            .map(normalize_loopback_redirect);\n\n        let mut oauth_config = load_oauth_config(&provider_name)\n            .ok_or_else(|| format!(\"no OAuth config for provider: {provider_name}\"))?;"
}

{
  "command": "bd create --title=\"Investigate MCP manual OAuth override being ignored (moltis.toml [mcp.servers.*.oauth])\" --description=\"Sub-complaint from GitHub issue #629: user reports that setting [mcp.servers.attio.oauth] in moltis.toml with client_id/auth_url/token_url/scopes does not suppress dynamic client registration — Moltis continues to perform RFC 7591 registration instead of using the manual client_id.\n\nCode review suggests the override path is wired: crates/mcp/src/manager.rs::build_auth_provider calls McpOAuthProvider::with_oauth_override when config.oauth is Some, and crates/mcp/src/auth.rs::start_web_oauth_flow's override branch skips discovery entirely. However, a repro is needed.\n\nHypotheses to investigate:\n- Servers added via the Web UI are stored in a separate registry (not moltis.toml) so a post-hoc TOML edit may not be merged into the running config.\n- config.oauth might be None for SSE servers even when the TOML has the section populated (schema parsing issue).\n- discovered_from:moltis-d7j\" --deps discovered-from:moltis-d7j --type=bug -p 2 --json 2>&1 | tail -15",
  "description": "File tracking bd for manual override investigation"
}

{
  "id": "moltis-c26",
  "title": "Investigate MCP manual OAuth override being ignored (moltis.toml [mcp.servers.*.oauth])",
  "description": "Sub-complaint from GitHub issue #629: user reports that setting [mcp.servers.attio.oauth] in moltis.toml with client_id/auth_url/token_url/scopes does not suppress dynamic client registration — Moltis continues to perform RFC 7591 registration instead of using the manual client_id.\n\nCode review suggests the override path is wired: crates/mcp/src/manager.rs::build_auth_provider calls McpOAuthProvider::with_oauth_override when config.oauth is Some, and crates/mcp/src/auth.rs::start_web_oauth_flow's override branch skips discovery entirely. However, a repro is needed.\n\nHypotheses to investigate:\n- Servers added via the Web UI are stored in a separate registry (not moltis.toml) so a post-hoc TOML edit may not be merged into the running config.\n- config.oauth might be None for SSE servers even when the TOML has the section populated (schema parsing issue).\n- discovered_from:moltis-d7j",

{

Providers without a\npre-registered redirect URI (e.g. custom OAuth 2.1 flows) now receive\n`http://localhost:<port>/auth/callback` at the authorization endpoint\ninstead of `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`, avoiding\n`invalid_redirect_uri` rejections from strict authorization servers.\nProviders with a baked-in redirect (openai-codex etc.) are unaffected.\n\nUnit tests (10) move with the helper to `crates/oauth/src/redirect.rs`.\nThe MCP-specific `start_web_oauth_flow` integration test stays in\n`crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -15",

{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"fix(mcp): normalize loopback OAuth redirect URIs to http (fixes #629)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Rewrite the OAuth redirect URI scheme from `https` to `http` when the host is a loopback identifier (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`). RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandates `http` for loopback redirects; strict authorization servers (Attio in #629) reject `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with `invalid_redirect_uri`.\n- The rewrite lives in `moltis_oauth::redirect::normalize_loopback_redirect` and is applied at two entry points: `McpOAuthProvider::start_web_oauth_flow` (fixes #629) and `LiveProviderSetupService::oauth_start` (proactive fix so provider OAuth can't hit the same trap).\n- **No changes to the callback handler, gateway service layer, or web UI.** The existing TLS-port HTTP peek/redirect in `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect` transparently bounces the plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS `/auth/callback` handler, preserving path and query string. `/auth/callback` is unauthenticated (no cookie needed), so the bounce is lossless.\n- Non-loopback hosts (`https://moltis.lan/auth/callback`, real TLS hostnames) are **untouched** — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect on a real hostname is a perfectly legitimate web-app redirect URI, unaffected by §8.3.\n\n## Why this shape\n\nTwo alternative designs were considered and rejected:\n\n1. **Ephemeral loopback callback server on `127.0.0.1:<random>`** (native-app pattern via the existing `CallbackServer`). Strict RFC 8252 compliance, but breaks deployments where Moltis serves a LAN hostname and the browser runs on a different host from the gateway — the ephemeral port on the Moltis host is unreachable.\n2. **Rewrite `redirect_uri` client-side in the UI.** Duplicates logic across `page-mcp.js`, `provider-oauth.js`, and onboarding; leaks TLS topology into the frontend.\n\nThe chosen approach — server-side scheme rewrite that relies on Moltis's own HTTP→HTTPS bounce — is minimal, backward compatible, and scales from single-host loopback to LAN-hostname deployments with zero branching.\n\n## Validation\n\n### Completed\n\n- [x] `cargo test -p moltis-oauth -p moltis-mcp -p moltis-provider-setup` — 10 new normalizer unit tests + 1 `start_web_oauth_flow` integration test (mockito) + 86 existing provider-setup tests; all 156+ tests pass\n- [x] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 fmt -p moltis-oauth -p moltis-mcp -p moltis-provider-setup -- --check`\n- [x] `cargo clippy -p moltis-oauth -p moltis-mcp -p moltis-provider-setup --all-targets -- -D warnings`\n- [x] `cargo check -p moltis-oauth -p moltis-mcp -p moltis-provider-setup`\n\n### Remaining\n\n- [ ] `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR>` (full workspace lint + test + macOS/iOS build gates)\n- [ ] `just release-preflight`\n\n## Manual QA\n\n1. Run Moltis on a TLS loopback deployment (`https://localhost:1455`).\n2. Settings → MCP Servers → Add Server → `https://mcp.attio.com/mcp`.\n3. Moltis receives 401, runs OAuth discovery, then POSTs to `https://app.attio.com/oauth/register`.\n4. **Expected:** registration body contains `\"redirect_uris\": [\"http://localhost:1455/auth/callback\"]` (scheme flipped to `http`).\n5. **Expected:** Attio returns 201 Created with a new `client_id` (no more `invalid_redirect_uri`).\n6. Browser opens Attio authorize URL; after consent, it redirects to `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback?code=...&state=...`.\n7. Moltis's TLS listener peek-redirects to `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback?code=...&state=...`.\n8. Browser lands on `oauth_callback_handler`; flow completes; MCP server transitions to Authenticated.\n\nNon-loopback deployments (`https://moltis.lan/auth/callback`) should see **no behavior change** — verify by re-adding a working server on that setup and confirming the registration body still uses the HTTPS form.\n\n## Out of scope (follow-ups)\n\n- **Manual `[mcp.servers.*.oauth]` override being ignored** (sub-complaint in #629). Code review of `build_auth_provider` + `start_web_oauth_flow`'s override branch suggests the plumbing is correct, but the reporter's claim needs a reproduction. Filed for investigation.\n\nFixes #629\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -20",
  "description": "Create pull request",
  "timeout": 120000
}

## Summary

**Two commits pushed to `boulder-penalty`:**

1. `033bb5fc` fix(mcp): normalize loopback redirect URIs to http for OAuth registration — core fix for #629, in `crates/mcp/src/auth.rs`
2. `86d1d09f` refactor(oauth): share loopback redirect normalizer and apply to provider setup — promotes helper to `moltis_oauth::redirect::normalize_loopback_redirect` and applies it in `crates/provider-setup/src/lib.rs::oauth_start`

**PR:** https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/636

**Validation passing locally:** `cargo test`, `cargo clippy -D warnings`, and `cargo +nightly fmt --check` across `moltis-oauth`, `moltis-mcp`, and `moltis-provider-setup`. 10 new unit tests for the normalizer (matrix: localhost / 127.0.0.1 / ::1 / case-insensitivity / non-loopback / http passthrough / query preservation / unparseable input) + 1 integration test driving `start_web_oauth_flow` through mockito.

**Follow-up tracked:** `moltis-c26` — investigate the manual `[mcp.servers.*.oauth]` override sub-complaint, which I couldn't reproduce without a live Attio connection and which the code path review suggests is already wired correctly.

    56→
    57→/// Load the OAuth config for a provider.
    58→///
    59→/// Priority:
    60→/// 1. User config file (`~/.config/moltis/oauth_providers.json`)
    61→/// 2. Environment variables (`MOLTIS_OAUTH_{PROVIDER}_CLIENT_ID`, etc.)
    62→/// 3. Built-in defaults
    63→pub fn load_oauth_config(provider: &str) -> Option<OAuthConfig> {
    64→    // Start from builtin defaults
    65→    let mut config = builtin_defaults().remove(provider)?;
    66→
    67→    // Override from config file

    69→        && let Ok(file_configs) = serde_json::from_str::<HashMap<String, OAuthConfig>>(&data)
    70→        && let Some(file_config) = file_configs.get(provider)
    71→    {
    72→        config = file_config.clone();
    73→    }

> AGENT

Good — `openai-codex` already uses `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback`, so the built-ins are fine. But `load_oauth_config` ALSO reads from `~/.config/moltis/oauth_providers.json` and `MOLTIS_OAUTH_*_REDIRECT_URI` env vars, which a user could set to `https://localhost:…`. Worse: `callback_port(&oauth_config)` parses from that URI, so a `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback` value would make `CallbackServer::wait_for_code` try to bind to Moltis's own port. Better to normalize the loaded config too.

> AGENT

Done. PR #636 updated with the Greptile P2 fix.

## What I changed

**Commit `4c1b2b18`** — normalize `oauth_config.redirect_uri` immediately after `load_oauth_config` in `LiveProviderSetupService::oauth_start`. This closes two real failure modes that Greptile's comment flagged:

1. **Port collision** — if a user sets `https://localhost:1455/auth/callback` in `~/.config/moltis/oauth_providers.json` or `MOLTIS_OAUTH_{PROVIDER}_REDIRECT_URI`, `callback_port` parses `1455` from the URI and `CallbackServer::wait_for_code` would try to bind against Moltis's main TLS listener.
2. **Strict AS rejection** — the `has_registered_redirect` branch would pass the HTTPS form through to the authorization request, which §8.3-enforcing servers reject.

Built-in `openai-codex` default is already `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback`, so no behavior change for the default path.

Also added a comment at the `has_registered_redirect` guard noting that pre-configured URIs are expected to already be loopback-normalized by that point (addresses the literal "add a brief comment" suggestion from Greptile too).

**Validation:** `cargo test -p moltis-provider-setup` (86 passed), format + clippy clean, pushed.

**Greptile reply posted:** https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/636#issuecomment-4222926635

CI is re-running against the new head commit; no other review feedback outstanding.

This protects against two real failure modes for users who override the built-in defaults via `~/.config/moltis/oauth_providers.json` or `MOLTIS_OAUTH_{PROVIDER}_REDIRECT_URI`:\n\n1. **Port collision** — `callback_port` would parse port `1455` from the HTTPS form and `CallbackServer::wait_for_code` would try to bind on Moltis's own main TLS port.\n2. **Strict AS rejection** — any authorization request using the pre-registered URI would still use the `https://` form and get rejected by §8.3-enforcing servers.\n\nThe built-in `openai-codex` default is unchanged (it already uses `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback`), so no behavior change there.     84→fn normalize_model_list(models: impl IntoIterator<Item = String>) -> Vec<String> {
    85→    let mut out = Vec::new();
    86→    for model in models {
    87→        let trimmed = model.trim();
    88→        if trimmed.is_empty() {
    89→            continue;
    90→        }
    91→        // Persist raw IDs so provider-local preferences don't collide with
    92→        // another provider's namespace (e.g. "openai::gpt-5.2").
    93→        let normalized = raw_model_id(trimmed).trim().to_string();
    94→        if normalized.is_empty() {
    95→            continue;
    96→        }
    97→        if out.iter().any(|existing| existing == &normalized) {
    98→            continue;
    99→        }
   100→        out.push(normalized);
   101→    }
   102→    out
   103→}
   104→
   105→fn parse_models_param(params: &Value) -> Vec<String> {
   106→    let from_array = params
   107→        .get("models")
   108→        .and_then(Value::as_array)
   109→        .map(|values| {
   110→            values
   111→                .iter()
   112→                .filter_map(Value::as_str)
   113→                .map(ToString::to_string)
   114→                .collect::<Vec<String>>()
   115→        })
   116→        .unwrap_or_default();
   117→
   118→    let mut models = normalize_model_list(from_array);
   119→    if models.is_empty()
   120→        && let Some(model) = params.get("model").and_then(Value::as_str)
   121→    {
   122→        models = normalize_model_list([model.to_string()]);
   123→    }
   124→    models
   125→}

  "new_string": "/// Normalize the `redirect_uri` on a provider `OAuthConfig` loaded via\n/// `load_oauth_config` so that loopback values always use the `http`\n/// scheme, per RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3.\n///\n/// Built-in defaults (e.g. `openai-codex`) already use\n/// `http://localhost:1455/auth/callback`, but `load_oauth_config` also\n/// reads from `~/.config/moltis/oauth_providers.json` and\n/// `MOLTIS_OAUTH_{PROVIDER}_REDIRECT_URI`, either of which could\n/// accidentally specify `https://localhost`. Without normalization:\n///\n/// * `callback_port` would parse the port from the HTTPS form and the\n///   spawned `CallbackServer` would try to bind on Moltis's main TLS\n///   port, which is already in use by the gateway.\n/// * Strict authorization servers would reject the authorization\n///   request with `invalid_redirect_uri`.\n///\n/// No-op for empty or non-loopback URIs.\nfn normalize_loaded_redirect_uri(config: &mut moltis_oauth::OAuthConfig) {\n    if config.redirect_uri.is_empty() {\n        return;\n    }\n    config.redirect_uri = normalize_loopback_redirect(&config.redirect_uri);\n}\n\nfn normalize_model_list(models: impl IntoIterator<Item = String>) -> Vec<String> {"
}

> AGENT

Done. 

## Summary

**Commit `2c6174e2`** — addresses Greptile's new P2 on PR #636 (config-file redirect normalization had no dedicated test).

- Extracted the inline `oauth_config.redirect_uri = normalize_loopback_redirect(...)` into a pure helper `normalize_loaded_redirect_uri(&mut OAuthConfig)` at `crates/provider-setup/src/lib.rs:84`.
- Added 7 unit tests in the existing `tests` module:
  1. `https://localhost` → `http://localhost`
  2. `https://127.0.0.1` → `http://127.0.0.1`
  3. `https://[::1]` → `http://[::1]`
  4. `http://` scheme preserved
  5. Real hostname (`moltis.lan`) preserved
  6. Empty string no-op
  7. Regression guard: `load_oauth_config("openai-codex")` + helper yields an http-loopback URI (exercises the full chain through the one built-in provider with a pre-registered redirect URI)

Rejected the alternative of env-var-based integration testing because `std::env::set_var` is process-global and would race other parallel tests.

**Results:** 93 tests in `moltis-provider-setup` (86 existing + 7 new), format + clippy clean, pushed, Greptile comment replied at https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/636#issuecomment-4223032852.
