```
     1→//! Redirect URI normalization helpers.
     2→//!
     3→//! Centralizes the RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 loopback rewrite so both MCP OAuth
     4→//! (RFC 7591 dynamic client registration) and provider OAuth produce
     5→//! authorization-server-compatible URIs regardless of the scheme the
     6→//! browser started on.
     7→
     8→use url::Url;
     9→
    10→/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs.
    11→///
    12→/// RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandate that loopback redirect URIs use the `http`
    13→/// scheme. Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject
    14→/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with
    15→/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization
    16→/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived
    17→/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.
    18→///
    19→/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see
    20→/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the
    21→/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,
    22→/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any
    23→/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host
    24→/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).
    25→///
    26→/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`
    27→/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect
    28→/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is
    29→/// unaffected by §8.3.
    30→#[must_use]
    31→pub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {
    32→    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {
    33→        return uri.to_string();
    34→    };
    35→    if parsed.scheme() != "https" {
    36→        return uri.to_string();
    37→    }
    38→    let is_loopback = match parsed.host() {
    39→        Some(url::Host::Domain(host)) => host.eq_ignore_ascii_case("localhost"),
    40→        Some(url::Host::Ipv4(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),
    41→        Some(url::Host::Ipv6(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),
    42→        None => false,
    43→    };
    44→    if !is_loopback {
    45→        return uri.to_string();
    46→    }
    47→    if parsed.set_scheme("http").is_err() {
    48→        return uri.to_string();
    49→    }
    50→    parsed.to_string()
    51→}
    52→
    53→#[cfg(test)]
    54→mod tests {
    55→    use super::*;
    56→
    57→    #[test]
    58→    fn rewrites_https_localhost() {
    59→        assert_eq!(
    60→            normalize_loopback_redirect("https://localhost:1455/auth/callback"),
    61→            "http://localhost:1455/auth/callback",
    62→        );
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    #[test]
    66→    fn rewrites_https_localhost_default_port() {
    67→        assert_eq!(
    68→            normalize_loopback_redirect("https://localhost/auth/callback"),
    69→            "http://localhost/auth/callback",
    70→        );
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    #[test]
    74→    fn rewrites_https_ipv4_loopback() {
    75→        assert_eq!(
    76→            normalize_loopback_redirect("https://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback"),
    77→            "http://127.0.0.1:1455/auth/callback",
    78→        );
    79→    }
    80→
    81→    #[test]
    82→    fn rewrites_https_ipv6_loopback() {
    83→        assert_eq!(
    84→            normalize_loopback_redirect("https://[::1]:1455/auth/callback"),
    85→            "http://[::1]:1455/auth/callback",

    91→        assert_eq!(
```

Looking at Codecov: 2 uncovered lines in `crates/oauth/src/redirect.rs` — likely the `None => false` match arm and the `set_scheme().is_err()` fallback. Both are defensive branches that are provably unreachable:
- `Url::parse("https://…")` always produces a URL with a host (special scheme), so `None` can't happen after the scheme check.
- `set_scheme` from `https`→`http` always succeeds (both are RFC 3986 special schemes with the same structure).

Let me simplify the function to remove the dead branches:

> TOOL

id: toolu_01McDnNu9awxU1Dw85zznJu9
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/boulder-penalty/crates/oauth/src/redirect.rs",
  "old_string": "use url::Url;\n\n/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs.\n///\n/// RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandate that loopback redirect URIs use the `http`\n/// scheme. Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with\n/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization\n/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived\n/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see\n/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the\n/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any\n/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host\n/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\n///\n/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`\n/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect\n/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is\n/// unaffected by §8.3.\n#[must_use]\npub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    let is_loopback = match parsed.host() {\n        Some(url::Host::Domain(host)) => host.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\"),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv4(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        Some(url::Host::Ipv6(ip)) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        None => false,\n    };\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    if parsed.set_scheme(\"http\").is_err() {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    parsed.to_string()\n}",
  "new_string": "use std::net::IpAddr;\n\nuse url::Url;\n\n/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs.\n///\n/// RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandate that loopback redirect URIs use the `http`\n/// scheme. Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with\n/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization\n/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived\n/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see\n/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the\n/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any\n/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host\n/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\n///\n/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`\n/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect\n/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is\n/// unaffected by §8.3.\n#[must_use]\npub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `https` is a \"special\" scheme in the WHATWG URL spec, so any Url\n    // with `scheme() == \"https\"` is guaranteed to have a host.\n    // `host_str` returns it without IPv6 brackets (so `[::1]` becomes\n    // `::1`), which lets us parse it uniformly as either `localhost` or\n    // an IP literal.\n    let Some(host) = parsed.host_str() else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    let is_loopback = host.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\")\n        || host\n            .parse::<IpAddr>()\n            .map(|ip| ip.is_loopback())\n            .unwrap_or(false);\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `set_scheme` only fails when changing between \"special\" and\n    // non-\"special\" schemes; `https`→`http` are both special and share\n    // the same structure, so this never fails in practice.\n    let _ = parsed.set_scheme(\"http\");\n    parsed.to_string()\n}"
}
```

`host_str` returns IPv6 with brackets (`[::1]`) which breaks `IpAddr::from_str`. Let me revert to `.host()` matching but use `is_some_and` so the `None` case doesn't need a dedicated match arm:

> TOOL

Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with\n/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization\n/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived\n/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see\n/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the\n/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any\n/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host\n/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\n///\n/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`\n/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect\n/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is\n/// unaffected by §8.3.\n#[must_use]\npub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `https` is a \"special\" scheme in the WHATWG URL spec, so any Url\n    // with `scheme() == \"https\"` is guaranteed to have a host.\n    // `host_str` returns it without IPv6 brackets (so `[::1]` becomes\n    // `::1`), which lets us parse it uniformly as either `localhost` or\n    // an IP literal.\n    let Some(host) = parsed.host_str() else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    let is_loopback = host.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\")\n        || host\n            .parse::<IpAddr>()\n            .map(|ip| ip.is_loopback())\n            .unwrap_or(false);\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `set_scheme` only fails when changing between \"special\" and\n    // non-\"special\" schemes; `https`→`http` are both special and share\n    // the same structure, so this never fails in practice.\n    let _ = parsed.set_scheme(\"http\");\n    parsed.to_string()\n}",
```json
  "new_string": "use url::{Host, Url};\n\n/// Rewrite `https://` → `http://` for loopback redirect URIs.\n///\n/// RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3 mandate that loopback redirect URIs use the `http`\n/// scheme. Strict authorization servers (e.g. Attio) reject\n/// `https://localhost` during RFC 7591 dynamic client registration with\n/// `invalid_redirect_uri`, and some also reject it during the authorization\n/// request. Moltis serves the web UI over TLS, so the origin-derived\n/// callback arrives as `https://localhost:<port>/auth/callback`.\n///\n/// The main TLS listener's peek-based HTTP→HTTPS redirect (see\n/// `moltis_tls::serve_tls_with_http_redirect`) transparently bounces the\n/// plain-HTTP OAuth callback back onto the real HTTPS callback handler,\n/// preserving path and query string, so this rewrite is safe for any\n/// deployment where the web UI is reached via a loopback host\n/// (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`).\n///\n/// Non-loopback hosts (`moltis.lan`, real TLS hostnames) and non-`https`\n/// schemes are returned unchanged — a confidential-client HTTPS redirect\n/// on a real hostname is a perfectly valid web-app redirect URI and is\n/// unaffected by §8.3.\n#[must_use]\npub fn normalize_loopback_redirect(uri: &str) -> String {\n    let Ok(mut parsed) = Url::parse(uri) else {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    };\n    if parsed.scheme() != \"https\" {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `https` is a \"special\" scheme in the WHATWG URL spec, so any Url\n    // with `scheme() == \"https\"` is guaranteed to have a host. Using\n    // `is_some_and` keeps the `None` branch in the std library rather\n    // than introducing an unreachable arm in our own code.\n    let is_loopback = parsed.host().is_some_and(|host| match host {\n        Host::Domain(domain) => domain.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"localhost\"),\n        Host::Ipv4(ip) => ip.is_loopback(),\n        Host::Ipv6(ip) => ip.is_loopback(),\n    });\n    if !is_loopback {\n        return uri.to_string();\n    }\n    // `set_scheme` only fails when changing between \"special\" and\n    // non-\"special\" schemes; `https`→`http` are both special and share\n    // the same structure, so this never fails in practice.\n    let _ = parsed.set_scheme(\"http\");\n    parsed.to_string()\n}"
}
```

## Summary

**Commit `3888d745`** — addresses the only new actionable feedback on PR #636 (Codecov's 98.5% patch coverage on `crates/oauth/src/redirect.rs`, 2 uncovered lines).

Both uncovered lines were defensive branches that are provably unreachable:

1. **`None => false`** in the host match — `https` is a WHATWG special scheme, so `Url::parse("https://…")` always produces a URL with a host. The earlier `scheme() == "https"` guard means `host()` is always `Some`.
2. **`set_scheme("http").is_err()` fallback** — `set_scheme` only fails when changing between special and non-special schemes; both `https` and `http` are special with the same structure.

Rather than inventing tests for unreachable code, I eliminated the dead branches:
- Replaced the host match with `parsed.host().is_some_and(|host| match host { ... })` — the `None` case now lives in the std library rather than as an untestable arm in our code.
- Switched `if parsed.set_scheme("http").is_err() { return ...; }` to `let _ = parsed.set_scheme("http");` with an explanatory comment.

**Other comments on the PR** (reviewed, no action needed):
- **Greptile** — still showing the previous P2 (last reviewed commit `4c1b2b18`); will re-review on the new push. My prior extraction to `normalize_loaded_redirect_uri` in commit `2c6174e2` already addressed it.
- **CodSpeed** — ×2.1 perf improvement on `env_substitution` benchmark (not attributable to this change; benign).
- **Codecov** — the target of this fix.

**Results:** 240 tests pass across `moltis-oauth` / `moltis-mcp` / `moltis-provider-setup`; format + clippy clean; pushed. PR now at commit `3888d745`.

## Summary

**Commit `14778cbf`** — addresses the one previously-missed Greptile P2 comment on the `normalize_loopback_redirect` review thread (auto-resolved earlier when my refactor touched the line, but the substantive suggestion was never implemented).

Added a `tracing::debug!` event that fires when the rewrite actually happens:

```rust
let _ = parsed.set_scheme("http");
let normalized = parsed.to_string();
debug!(
    original = %uri,
    normalized = %normalized,
    "rewrote loopback OAuth redirect URI from https to http (RFC 8252 §7.3/§8.3)"
);
normalized
```

Why debug level: the rewrite fires at most once per OAuth flow, so it's not log-spam at info, but operators need to be able to verify the fix is active when diagnosing downstream rejections. `RUST_LOG=moltis_oauth::redirect=debug` enables it. Redirect URIs aren't sensitive (public callback endpoint, not credentials), so full-value logging at debug is safe.

**PR state:**
- 10 redirect tests pass, format + clippy clean
- Pushed to `boulder-penalty` (`14778cbf`)
- Review thread `PRRT_kwDOREW6tc56GvXZ` resolved with reply summarizing the fix
- All review threads now `isResolved: true`
